   736	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   737	# External reference roots (PR-3, external-citation-base-spec-20260727.md
   738	# §2.2-2.5). Everything from here to `resolve_external_citation` implements
   739	# the second, structurally separate resolution domain `@@<alias>/<relpath>`
   740	# citations use. See the module docstring's top for the one-paragraph
   741	# orientation and `verify_round` for how this is wired into Rule B.
   742	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   743	
   744	
   745	class ReferenceRoot:
   746	    """One declared external reference root, fully resolved (or definitively
   747	    marked unavailable) for this run.
   748	
   749	    Built once per `verify_project` call by `load_reference_roots` — never
   750	    re-derived per-citation or per-round; `verify_round` receives the same
   751	    `dict[str, ReferenceRoot]` for every round in the project (G5's "every
   752	    run re-validates" applies at the `load_reference_roots` call granularity
   753	    — once per `verify_project` invocation — not once per lookup within it,
   754	    and not cached *across* separate invocations).
   755	
   756	    `canonical` is `None` unless `available` is `True` — every caller must
   757	    check `available` before touching `canonical`, never the reverse.
   758	    `unavailable_reason` is one of `"unbound"`, `"unresolvable"`,
   759	    `"rejected"`, `"identity-mismatch"`, `"shadow-alias"`, or `None` (only
   760	    when available) —
   761	    deliberately a short enum string, never a path (G20: nothing derived
   762	    from this object may leak an absolute path into a violation detail,
   763	    coverage line, or default `--json` output).
   764	    """
   765	
   766	    __slots__ = (
   767	        "alias",
   768	        "purpose",
   769	        "expect_present",
   770	        "subpaths",
   771	        "approved_by",
   772	        "canonical",
   773	        "available",
   774	        "unavailable_reason",
   775	    )
   776	
   777	    def __init__(
   778	        self,
   779	        alias: str,
   780	        purpose: str,
   781	        expect_present: tuple[str, ...],
   782	        subpaths: tuple[str, ...] | None,
   783	        approved_by: str,
   784	        canonical: Path | None,
   785	        available: bool,
   786	        unavailable_reason: str | None,
   787	    ) -> None:
   788	        self.alias = alias
   789	        self.purpose = purpose
   790	        self.expect_present = expect_present
   791	        self.subpaths = subpaths
   792	        self.approved_by = approved_by
   793	        self.canonical = canonical
   794	        self.available = available
   795	        self.unavailable_reason = unavailable_reason
   796	
   797	
   798	def _load_versioned_roots(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], str | None]:
   799	    """Parse and fully validate `.harnessloop/setup/reference-roots.json`.
   800	
   801	    Returns `(entries, None)` on success (`entries` is a list of plain dicts,
   802	    one per declared root, with keys already normalized to
   803	    `tuple[str, ...]` where the schema calls for a list) or `([], message)`
   804	    on ANY structural problem (G1): a single bad root entry invalidates the
   805	    *entire* file — "整份作废、加载零个 root" (§2.2) — deliberately not a
   806	    partial load, so a project can never end up in an ambiguous "some of my
   807	    declared roots loaded, which ones?" state.
   808	    """
   809	    try:
   810	        size = path.stat().st_size
   811	    except OSError as exc:
   812	        return [], f"{path}: cannot stat ({exc})"
   813	    if size > REFERENCE_ROOTS_MAX_BYTES:
   814	        return [], f"{path}: exceeds {REFERENCE_ROOTS_MAX_BYTES} bytes"
   815	    try:
   816	        text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   817	    except OSError as exc:
   818	        return [], f"{path}: cannot read ({exc})"
   819	    try:
   820	        data = json.loads(text)
   821	    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
   822	        return [], f"{path}: invalid JSON ({exc})"
   823	    if not isinstance(data, dict):
   824	        return [], f"{path}: top level must be a JSON object"
   825	    unknown_top = set(data) - {"version", "roots"}
   826	    if unknown_top:
   827	        return [], f"{path}: unknown top-level key(s) {sorted(unknown_top)}"
   828	    if data.get("version") != 1:
   829	        return [], f"{path}: 'version' must be 1"
   830	    raw_roots = data.get("roots", [])
   831	    if not isinstance(raw_roots, list):
   832	        return [], f"{path}: 'roots' must be a list"
   833	    if len(raw_roots) > REFERENCE_ROOTS_MAX_COUNT:
   834	        return [], f"{path}: more than {REFERENCE_ROOTS_MAX_COUNT} roots declared"
   835	
   836	    # G1: an alias may not collide with a protocol PATHISH_PREFIXES token
   837	    # (e.g. an alias literally named "setup" or "state"), computed from the
   838	    # same module-level constant Rule B's own base resolution uses so the
   839	    # two lists can never drift apart.
   840	    pathish_tokens = {p.strip("./").rstrip("/") for p in PATHISH_PREFIXES}
   841	
   842	    seen_alias: set[str] = set()
   843	    entries: list[dict] = []
   844	    for i, raw in enumerate(raw_roots):
   845	        if not isinstance(raw, dict):
   846	            return [], f"{path}: roots[{i}] must be an object"
   847	        unknown = set(raw) - _VERSIONED_ROOT_ALLOWED_KEYS
   848	        if unknown:
   849	            return [], f"{path}: roots[{i}] has unknown key(s) {sorted(unknown)}"
   850	
   851	        alias = raw.get("alias")
   852	        if not isinstance(alias, str) or not ALIAS_RE.match(alias):
   853	            return [], f"{path}: roots[{i}].alias is missing or does not match {ALIAS_RE.pattern}"
   854	        if alias in pathish_tokens:
   855	            return [], f"{path}: roots[{i}].alias {alias!r} collides with a PATHISH_PREFIXES token"
   856	        if alias in seen_alias:
   857	            return [], f"{path}: duplicate alias {alias!r}"
   858	        seen_alias.add(alias)
   859	
   860	        purpose = raw.get("purpose")
   861	        if not isinstance(purpose, str) or not purpose.strip():
   862	            return [], f"{path}: roots[{i}].purpose must be a non-empty string"
   863	
   864	        expect_present = raw.get("expect_present")
   865	        if (
   866	            not isinstance(expect_present, list)
   867	            or not (1 <= len(expect_present) <= 8)
   868	            or not all(isinstance(p, str) and p.strip() for p in expect_present)
   869	        ):
   870	            return [], f"{path}: roots[{i}].expect_present must be 1-8 non-empty strings"
   871	
   872	        subpaths = raw.get("subpaths")
   873	        if subpaths is not None and (
   874	            not isinstance(subpaths, list)
   875	            or not all(isinstance(p, str) and p.strip() and "/" not in p for p in subpaths)
   876	        ):
   877	            return [], f"{path}: roots[{i}].subpaths, if present, must be single-segment names"
   878	
   879	        approved_by = raw.get("approved_by")
   880	        if not isinstance(approved_by, str) or not approved_by.strip():
   881	            return [], f"{path}: roots[{i}].approved_by must be a non-empty string"
   882	
   883	        entries.append(
   884	            {
   885	                "alias": alias,
   886	                "purpose": purpose,
   887	                "expect_present": tuple(expect_present),
   888	                "subpaths": tuple(subpaths) if subpaths is not None else None,
   889	                "approved_by": approved_by,
   890	            }
   891	        )
   892	    return entries, None
   893	
   894	
   895	def _load_local_bindings(path: Path) -> tuple[dict[str, str], str | None]:
   896	    """Parse and validate `.harnessloop/local/reference-roots.local.json`.
   897	
   898	    Returns `(bindings, None)` where `bindings` maps alias -> raw (not yet
   899	    expanded or resolved) declared path string, or `({}, message)` on any
   900	    structural problem (G2/G3). A binding entry declaring any of
   901	    `_LOCAL_BINDING_FORBIDDEN_KEYS` (`identity`/`available`/`optional`/
   902	    `expect_present`) is rejected specifically because those keys would let
   903	    the low-trust local side answer a high-trust question ("is this the
   904	    right tree") that only the project-committed sentinel check
   905	    (`expect_present`, verified server-side by `_load_one_root`) is allowed
   906	    to answer (§2.2: "低信任半边不得影响可用性判定").
   907	
   908	    A missing file is not an error — every declared alias is simply
   909	    `"unbound"` (see `_load_one_root`), which is the correct default: a
   910	    fresh clone or a new machine has bound nothing yet.
   911	    """
   912	    if not path.is_file():
   913	        return {}, None
   914	    try:
   915	        size = path.stat().st_size
   916	    except OSError as exc:
   917	        return {}, f"{path}: cannot stat ({exc})"
   918	    if size > REFERENCE_ROOTS_MAX_BYTES:
   919	        return {}, f"{path}: exceeds {REFERENCE_ROOTS_MAX_BYTES} bytes"
   920	    try:
   921	        text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   922	    except OSError as exc:
   923	        return {}, f"{path}: cannot read ({exc})"
   924	    try:
   925	        data = json.loads(text)
   926	    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
   927	        return {}, f"{path}: invalid JSON ({exc})"
   928	    if not isinstance(data, dict):
   929	        return {}, f"{path}: top level must be a JSON object"
   930	    unknown_top = set(data) - {"version", "bindings"}
   931	    if unknown_top:
   932	        return {}, f"{path}: unknown top-level key(s) {sorted(unknown_top)}"
   933	    if data.get("version") != 1:
   934	        return {}, f"{path}: 'version' must be 1"
   935	    raw_bindings = data.get("bindings", {})
   936	    if not isinstance(raw_bindings, dict):
   937	        return {}, f"{path}: 'bindings' must be an object"
   938	
   939	    bindings: dict[str, str] = {}
   940	    for alias, binding in raw_bindings.items():
   941	        if not isinstance(binding, dict):
   942	            return {}, f"{path}: bindings[{alias!r}] must be an object"
   943	        forbidden = _LOCAL_BINDING_FORBIDDEN_KEYS & set(binding)
   944	        if forbidden:
   945	            return {}, (
   946	                f"{path}: bindings[{alias!r}] declares low-trust identity "
   947	                f"key(s) {sorted(forbidden)} -- the local binding file may "
   948	                "only ever answer 'where', never 'is this the right tree'"
   949	            )
   950	        unknown = set(binding) - _LOCAL_BINDING_ALLOWED_KEYS
   951	        if unknown:
   952	            return {}, f"{path}: bindings[{alias!r}] has unknown key(s) {sorted(unknown)}"
   953	        raw_path = binding.get("path")
   954	        if not isinstance(raw_path, str) or not raw_path.strip():
   955	            return {}, f"{path}: bindings[{alias!r}].path must be a non-empty string"
   956	        bindings[alias] = raw_path
   957	    return bindings, None
   958	
   959	
   960	def _resolve_in_root(root: Path, rel: str) -> Path | None:
   961	    """PR-3 §2.5's two orthogonal containment defenses, applied to an
   962	    external reference root: `root` must already be canonical (this is the
   963	    same "pinned domain" `_is_contained_pinned` requires — see its
   964	    docstring).
   965	
   966	    Defense 1 — literal traversal rejection, applied to the raw text before
   967	    any join: an empty `rel`, or one starting with `/` or `~`, a Windows
   968	    drive-absolute form, or containing a `..` segment, is rejected before
   969	    ever touching the filesystem (mirrors `_looks_like_out_of_project` /
   970	    the project-domain `..` rejection in `_resolve_in_project` — the same
   971	    escape shapes are just as real against an external root as against the
   972	    project).
   973	
   974	    Defense 2 — unfolded raw join + pinned canonical containment: the
   975	    candidate is `root / rel`, never a pre-folded
   976	    `Path(os.path.normpath(root / rel))` copy of it (T-064 MUST-FIX C,
   977	    reapplied to the new domain: folding `link/../escape.md` lexically
   978	    *before* resolving would erase the very symlink hop containment exists
   979	    to catch). `_is_contained_pinned` resolves the raw join and checks it
   980	    against the domain pinned at load time.
   981	    """
   982	    if (
   983	        not rel
   984	        or rel.startswith(("/", "~"))
   985	        or WINDOWS_DRIVE_ABS_RE.match(rel)
   986	        or any(seg == ".." for seg in rel.split("/"))
   987	    ):
   988	        return None
   989	    candidate = root / rel
   990	    if not _is_contained_pinned(candidate, root):
   991	        return None
   992	    return candidate
   993	
   994	
   995	def _resolve_case_exact(root: Path, rel: str) -> Path | None:
   996	    """Walk `rel`'s segments under `root` one directory entry at a time via
   997	    `os.scandir`, matching each segment name *exactly* (byte-for-byte
   998	    string comparison), never through `Path.exists()`/`Path.is_dir()` --
   999	    G10: those two delegate to the host filesystem's own comparison, which
  1000	    is case-*insensitive* (but case-preserving) on a default macOS or
  1001	    Windows filesystem, so a citation with the wrong case for a real file
  1002	    would otherwise resolve there (measured directly against this project's
  1003	    own external root: `@@wiki/KERNEL/FACTS.MD` resolves via `.exists()` on
  1004	    macOS even though the real file is `kernel/facts.md`).
  1005	
  1006	    Deliberately host-independent and never delegated to `resolve()`: this
  1007	    function's whole job is to disagree with the host filesystem's own
  1008	    case-folding when they differ, so it cannot reuse anything that follows
  1009	    that folding.
  1010	
  1011	    Returns the real, exact-case path if every segment matched exactly, or
  1012	    `None` the moment any segment does not (missing entry, or `root`/an
  1013	    intermediate segment is not actually a directory). A caller still owns
  1014	    the final existence/directory-semantics decision (`_exists_as`, called
  1015	    by `resolve_external_citation`) — this function only proves "an entry
  1016	    with this exact name exists at this level", not "the whole path is
  1017	    usable the way the citation implies" (e.g. a broken symlink's final
  1018	    segment is still found here; `resolve_external_citation` is what turns
  1019	    that into `not_found`, exactly as `_exists_as` does for the project
  1020	    domain).
  1021	    """
  1022	    current = root
  1023	    for seg in (s for s in rel.split("/") if s):
  1024	        if not current.is_dir():
  1025	            return None
  1026	        try:
  1027	            with os.scandir(current) as it:
  1028	                match = next((e for e in it if e.name == seg), None)
  1029	        except OSError:
  1030	            return None
  1031	        if match is None:
  1032	            return None
  1033	        current = current / seg
  1034	    return current
  1035	
  1036	
  1037	def resolve_external_citation(root: ReferenceRoot, rel: str) -> tuple[str, Path | None]:
  1038	    """Resolve `rel` (the text after `@@<alias>/`, one attempt — the caller
  1039	    tries the as-written form and, on failure, a locator-stripped retry, the
  1040	    same order Rule B's project-domain resolution uses) against
  1041	    `root.canonical`.
  1042	
  1043	    Returns `("resolved" | "not_found" | "rejected", path-or-None)`.
  1044	    `root` must already be `available` — callers check that separately and
  1045	    report `external-citation-unverifiable` instead of ever calling this
  1046	    (G7); this function has no "unavailable" outcome of its own.
  1047	
  1048	    Order: (1) `_resolve_in_root` — G8's literal-traversal defense and G9's
  1049	    unfolded-join pinned containment; a rejection here is final and never
  1050	    falls through to the existence check below (a rejected `candidate` is
  1051	    not something `_resolve_case_exact` should ever be asked about). (2)
  1052	    `_resolve_case_exact` — G10's exact-case segment walk; `None` here is
  1053	    `not_found`. (3) directory-semantics existence (G11): `is_dir()` when
  1054	    `rel` ends in `/`, `exists()` otherwise (`exists()` follows symlinks, so
  1055	    a broken symlink's dangling target is `not_found`, not `resolved`,
  1056	    exactly like the project domain's `_exists_as`). (4) `subpaths`
  1057	    whitelist (G12), checked against the *canonical* path relative to the
  1058	    canonical root, not the literal `rel` — a `kernel/link -> <root>/raw`
  1059	    symlink cited as `kernel/link/x.md` must be judged by where it actually
  1060	    lands (`raw`), not by the literal first segment it was typed with.
  1061	    """
  1062	    candidate = _resolve_in_root(root.canonical, rel)
  1063	    if candidate is None:
  1064	        return "rejected", None
  1065	
  1066	    want_dir = rel.endswith("/")
  1067	    real = _resolve_case_exact(root.canonical, rel)
  1068	    if real is None:
  1069	        return "not_found", None
  1070	    if not _exists_as(real, want_dir):
  1071	        return "not_found", None
  1072	
  1073	    if root.subpaths:
  1074	        canon_real = _canonical(real)
  1075	        try:
  1076	            rel_canon = canon_real.relative_to(root.canonical)
  1077	        except ValueError:
  1078	            return "rejected", None
  1079	        first_seg = rel_canon.parts[0] if rel_canon.parts else None
  1080	        if first_seg not in root.subpaths:
  1081	            return "rejected", None
  1082	
  1083	    return "resolved", real
  1084	
  1085	
  1086	def _resolve_external_with_locator(root: ReferenceRoot, rel: str, full_cited: str) -> str:
  1087	    """Try `rel` as written; on failure, retry with `full_cited`'s own
  1088	    trailing locator suffix stripped (mirrors Rule B's project-domain
  1089	    "original, then locator-stripped" order — see `strip_locator_suffix`,
  1090	    unchanged by PR-3 and already verified to work unmodified against
  1091	    `@@alias/...` spans).
  1092	
  1093	    Returns only the outcome string (`"resolved" | "not_found" |
  1094	    "rejected"`) since the specific resolved path is not needed by the
  1095	    citation-existence caller (unlike the sentinel/subpaths checks, which do
  1096	    need it). If neither attempt resolves, `"rejected"` wins over
  1097	    `"not_found"` so a genuinely malformed or escaping relpath is never
  1098	    silently downgraded to a plain "no such file" just because a
  1099	    locator-stripped retry also failed to find anything.
  1100	    """
  1101	    outcome, _ = resolve_external_citation(root, rel)
  1102	    if outcome == "resolved":
  1103	        return outcome
  1104	    stripped_full = strip_locator_suffix(full_cited)
  1105	    if stripped_full != full_cited:
  1106	        m = ALIAS_CITATION_RE.match(stripped_full)
  1107	        if m and m.group(2) != rel:
  1108	            outcome2, _ = resolve_external_citation(root, m.group(2))
  1109	            if outcome2 == "resolved":
  1110	                return outcome2
  1111	            if outcome2 == "rejected":
  1112	                outcome = "rejected"
  1113	    return outcome
  1114	
  1115	
  1116	def _load_one_root(
  1117	    entry: dict, raw_path: str | None, project_canonical: Path, verify_identity: bool
  1118	) -> ReferenceRoot:
  1119	    """Resolve one declared root entry to a `ReferenceRoot` (G4-G6).
  1120	
  1121	    Load order is itself the safety property (§2.5): `expanduser` (`~` only,
  1122	    no env/glob interpolation) -> `resolve(strict=True)` (wrapped in
  1123	    `try/except (OSError, RuntimeError)` — a symlink loop raises
  1124	    `RuntimeError`, not `OSError`, on the interpreters this was measured
  1125	    against) -> `is_dir()` -> *only then* the forbidden-root checks, and
  1126	    every one of those checks compares **canonical** values, never the raw
  1127	    declared string (G4: resolving first is what stops a symlink like
  1128	    `fakehome/w2 -> <project's own parent directory>` — which reads as an
  1129	    innocuous relative string — from defeating every literal check that ran
  1130	    before resolution). The declared-string glob/env-character check is the
  1131	    one exception: it runs on the raw string, deliberately before
  1132	    `expanduser`/`resolve` are ever called, since a literal `*`/`$(`/`${`
  1133	    in a path meant to be used literally is suspicious independent of what
  1134	    it might canonicalize to.
  1135	    """
  1136	    common = dict(
  1137	        alias=entry["alias"],
  1138	        purpose=entry["purpose"],
  1139	        expect_present=entry["expect_present"],
  1140	        subpaths=entry["subpaths"],
  1141	        approved_by=entry["approved_by"],
  1142	    )
  1143	
  1144	    if raw_path is None:
  1145	        return ReferenceRoot(canonical=None, available=False, unavailable_reason="unbound", **common)
  1146	
  1147	    if any(ch in _GLOB_OR_ENV_CHARS for ch in raw_path) or "${" in raw_path or "$(" in raw_path:
  1148	        return ReferenceRoot(canonical=None, available=False, unavailable_reason="rejected", **common)
  1149	
  1150	    try:
  1151	        canonical = Path(raw_path).expanduser().resolve(strict=True)
  1152	    except (OSError, RuntimeError):
  1153	        return ReferenceRoot(canonical=None, available=False, unavailable_reason="unresolvable", **common)
  1154	
  1155	    if not canonical.is_dir():
  1156	        return ReferenceRoot(canonical=None, available=False, unavailable_reason="rejected", **common)
  1157	
  1158	    home = Path.home().resolve()
  1159	    forbidden = (
  1160	        canonical == Path(canonical.anchor)
  1161	        or canonical == home
  1162	        or canonical == home.parent
  1163	        or is_under(project_canonical, canonical)  # root is project's ancestor or itself
  1164	        or is_under(canonical, project_canonical)  # root sits inside the project
  1165	    )
  1166	    if forbidden:
  1167	        return ReferenceRoot(canonical=None, available=False, unavailable_reason="rejected", **common)
  1168	
  1169	    if verify_identity:
  1170	        for sentinel in entry["expect_present"]:
  1171	            candidate = _resolve_in_root(canonical, sentinel)
  1172	            if candidate is None or not _exists_as(candidate, sentinel.endswith("/")):
  1173	                return ReferenceRoot(
  1174	                    canonical=None, available=False, unavailable_reason="identity-mismatch", **common
  1175	                )
  1176	
  1177	    return ReferenceRoot(canonical=canonical, available=True, unavailable_reason=None, **common)
  1178	
  1179	
  1180	def load_reference_roots(
  1181	    project: Path, verify_identity: bool = True
  1182	) -> tuple[dict[str, ReferenceRoot], list[dict]]:
  1183	    """Load, validate, and resolve every declared external reference root.
  1184	
  1185	    Reads two files: the versioned, zero-absolute-path
  1186	    `.harnessloop/setup/reference-roots.json` (git-committed) and the
  1187	    gitignored, machine-local `.harnessloop/local/reference-roots.local.json`
  1188	    (§2.2). Absence of the versioned file is treated exactly like an empty
  1189	    `roots` list — today's behavior, unchanged (§2.2: "缺席 ≡ 空列表 ≡ 今天的
  1190	    行为"). This is the *only* parser for either file — `check_setup.py`
  1191	    must call this (with `verify_identity=False`, see below) rather than
  1192	    writing a second one if it ever wants to report an advisory line (§2.2:
  1193	    "不得自写第二个解析器").
  1194	
  1195	    `verify_identity`: when `True` (the default, and always `True` when
  1196	    called from `verify_project`), a root whose `expect_present` sentinels
  1197	    do not all resolve is marked unavailable
  1198	    (`unavailable_reason="identity-mismatch"`) rather than available. When
  1199	    `False`, that check is skipped entirely — intended for a future
  1200	    optimistic/advisory reader (e.g. a setup wizard reporting "N reference
  1201	    roots declared") that must never let a low-cost, best-effort read
  1202	    silently disagree with what the mechanical gate would decide; skipping
  1203	    the check is the honest way to do that (reporting a wrong "available"
  1204	    would not be).
  1205	
  1206	    Returns `(roots, violations)`. `roots` maps alias -> `ReferenceRoot`
  1207	    (both available and unavailable ones — callers distinguish via
  1208	    `.available`). `violations` are the G1/G2/G4/G5/G6 declaration-level
  1209	    problems found while loading — never round-scoped (a declaration is a
  1210	    project-level fact), so every violation here carries `"round":
  1211	    str(project)`. `G7`'s per-alias-per-round-independent
  1212	    `external-root-unavailable` violation is *not* added here — that is the
  1213	    caller's (`verify_project`'s) job, exactly once per unavailable alias,
  1214	    project-wide (§2.7: "external_roots_declared/available ... 项目级,在轮次
  1215	    循环之后单次赋值").
  1216	    """
  1217	    violations: list[dict] = []
  1218	    versioned_path = project / REFERENCE_ROOTS_VERSIONED_REL
  1219	    local_path = project / REFERENCE_ROOTS_LOCAL_REL
  1220	
  1221	    if not versioned_path.is_file():
  1222	        return {}, violations
  1223	
  1224	    entries, versioned_error = _load_versioned_roots(versioned_path)
  1225	    if versioned_error is not None:
  1226	        violations.append(
  1227	            {"round": str(project), "kind": "reference-roots-invalid", "detail": versioned_error}
  1228	        )
  1229	        return {}, violations
  1230	
  1231	    bindings, local_error = _load_local_bindings(local_path)
  1232	    if local_error is not None:
  1233	        violations.append(
  1234	            {"round": str(project), "kind": "reference-root-local-invalid", "detail": local_error}
  1235	        )
  1236	        bindings = {}
  1237	
  1238	    project_canonical = _canonical(project)
  1239	    roots: dict[str, ReferenceRoot] = {}
  1240	    for entry in entries:
  1241	        alias = entry["alias"]
  1242	        root = _load_one_root(entry, bindings.get(alias), project_canonical, verify_identity)
  1243	        roots[alias] = root
  1244	        if root.unavailable_reason == "rejected":
  1245	            violations.append(
  1246	                {
  1247	                    "round": str(project),
  1248	                    "kind": "reference-root-rejected",
  1249	                    "detail": (
  1250	                        f"reference root '{alias}' is rejected (forbidden location, "
  1251	                        "not a directory, or a glob/env character in its declared path)"
  1252	                    ),
  1253	                }
  1254	            )
  1255	        elif root.unavailable_reason == "unresolvable":
  1256	            violations.append(
  1257	                {
  1258	                    "round": str(project),
  1259	                    "kind": "reference-root-unresolvable",
  1260	                    "detail": f"reference root '{alias}' could not be resolved (symlink loop or OS error)",
  1261	                }
  1262	            )
  1263	        elif root.unavailable_reason == "identity-mismatch":
  1264	            violations.append(
  1265	                {
  1266	                    "round": str(project),
  1267	                    "kind": "reference-root-identity-mismatch",
  1268	                    "detail": (
  1269	                        f"reference root '{alias}' is bound but at least one of its "
  1270	                        "declared `expect_present` sentinels does not exist there"
  1271	                    ),
  1272	                }
  1273	            )
  1274	        # "unbound" produces no G1-G6 violation here -- only the G7
  1275	        # `external-root-unavailable` violation the caller adds once,
  1276	        # project-wide, for every unavailable alias regardless of reason.
  1277	
  1278	    # G21 (shadow alias, §2.4 "禁止两 alias 指向同一 canonical root"): two
  1279	    # aliases resolving to the same canonical root is an audit-bypass face,
  1280	    # not a convenience. `reference-roots.json` is the tracked, reviewable
  1281	    # record of *what external trees this project reads*; a second alias
  1282	    # bound to an already-declared tree lets a citation read that tree under
  1283	    # a name whose `purpose`/`approved_by` were never reviewed for it.
  1284	    #
  1285	    # This must run here rather than in `_load_versioned_roots`: the tracked
  1286	    # file holds no paths at all (§2.2 zero-absolute-path), so "same root"
  1287	    # is only knowable after local binding + canonical resolution. Comparing
  1288	    # the declared strings would be the string-comparison mistake G4 already
  1289	    # has teeth against -- two different literals (one a symlink to the
  1290	    # other, `.` / `..` segments, a trailing slash) can name one directory.
  1291	    #
  1292	    # Fail-closed: every alias in a colliding group is marked unavailable,
  1293	    # matching how `rejected`/`identity-mismatch` behave. Silently keeping
  1294	    # one of them (say, the first) would make which alias survives depend on
  1295	    # declaration order -- the same "shape decides the outcome at runtime"
  1296	    # anti-pattern §2.4's last paragraph forbids.
  1297	    by_canonical: dict[Path, list[str]] = {}
  1298	    for alias, root in roots.items():
  1299	        if root.available and root.canonical is not None:
  1300	            by_canonical.setdefault(root.canonical, []).append(alias)
  1301	    for group in sorted(
  1302	        (sorted(a) for a in by_canonical.values() if len(a) > 1),
  1303	        key=lambda g: g[0],
  1304	    ):
  1305	        for alias in group:
  1306	            old = roots[alias]
  1307	            roots[alias] = ReferenceRoot(
  1308	                alias=old.alias,
  1309	                purpose=old.purpose,
  1310	                expect_present=old.expect_present,
  1311	                subpaths=old.subpaths,
  1312	                approved_by=old.approved_by,
  1313	                canonical=None,
  1314	                available=False,
  1315	                unavailable_reason="shadow-alias",
  1316	            )
  1317	        violations.append(
  1318	            {
  1319	                "round": str(project),
  1320	                "kind": "reference-root-shadow-alias",
  1321	                # G20: aliases only -- naming the shared directory here would
  1322	                # leak an absolute host path into a violation detail.
  1323	                "detail": (
  1324	                    f"reference roots {group} resolve to the same canonical root "
  1325	                    "(shadow alias; forbidden by the one-alias-one-root rule); "
  1326	                    "all of them are marked unavailable"
  1327	                ),
  1328	            }
  1329	        )
  1330	    return roots, violations
  1331	
  1332	
  1333	def submodule_roots(project: Path) -> list[Path]:
  1334	    """Every git submodule directory declared in .gitmodules, at any depth.
  1335	
  2035	        if entry.is_file():
  2036	            files.append(entry)
  2037	    return files, violations
  2038	
  2039	
  2040	def verify_round(
  2041	    project: Path,
  2042	    round_dir: Path,
  2043	    suffix_index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]],
  2044	    roots: dict[str, "ReferenceRoot"],
  2045	) -> tuple[list[dict], dict]:
  2046	    violations: list[dict] = []
  2047	    goal_dir = round_dir.parent.parent
  2048	    bases = [project, goal_dir, round_dir]
  2049	    # Rule B only: extra resolution bases for (1) citations that use one of
  2050	    # the PATHISH_PREFIXES verbatim (setup/, state/, goals/, ...) — those
  2051	    # directories actually live under <project>/.harnessloop/, which none of
  2052	    # `bases` covers on their own — and (2) citations written relative to a
  2053	    # submodule's own root. Kept separate from `bases` so Rule A's
  2054	    # scope-lock containment check is not loosened by either addition.
  2055	    citation_bases = bases + [project / ".harnessloop"] + submodule_roots(project)
  2056	
  2057	    coverage = _empty_coverage()
  2058	    coverage["rounds"] = 1
  2059	
  2060	    # G17 item 1 (external-citation-base-spec-20260727.md §3.1): check
  2061	    # evidence/ and reviews/ containment *before* either is ever listed —
  2062	    # `goals_dir` / a `goal_dir` / this `round_dir` were already checked the
  2063	    # same way by the caller (`verify_project`) before `verify_round` was
  2064	    # even invoked, so by this point only these two remaining containers in
  2065	    # the chain are unverified. A container whose check fails here is never
  2066	    # enumerated at all (no `rglob`, no `.iterdir()`) — its files are simply
  2067	    # absent from `sub_files`/`checked_files`, not silently emptied by some
  2068	    # later filter.
  2069	    sub_files: dict[str, list[Path]] = {"evidence": [], "reviews": []}
  2070	    sub_containers: dict[str, Path | None] = {}
  2071	    for sub in ("evidence", "reviews"):
  2072	        container = round_dir / sub
  2073	        escape = _container_escape_violation(container, project, round_dir)
  2074	        if escape is not None:
  2075	            violations.append(escape)
  2076	            sub_containers[sub] = None
  2077	            continue
  2078	        sub_containers[sub] = container
  2079	        if container.is_dir():
  2080	            files, artifact_violations = _scan_round_artifacts(container, project, round_dir)
  2081	            sub_files[sub] = files
  2082	            violations.extend(artifact_violations)
  2083	
  2084	    checked_files = sub_files["evidence"] + sub_files["reviews"]
  2085	    if not checked_files:
  2086	        coverage["rounds_zero_inspected"] = 1
  2087	
  2088	    # E2(a): scope-lock existence and Allowed-Changes parseability are
  2089	    # checked unconditionally for every round, independent of whether the
  2090	    # round has any evidence/review files. Before this change both checks
  2091	    # lived behind `if checked_files:`, so a round with nothing under
  2092	    # evidence/ or reviews/ never had its scope-lock inspected at all (the
  2093	    # historical "9 rounds zero-inspected, still exit 0" gap). Containment
  2094	    # (below) still requires artifacts to check, so it stays guarded.
  2095	    scope_lock = round_dir / "scope-lock.md"
  2096	    spans: list[str] = []
  2097	    if not scope_lock.exists():
  2098	        violations.append(
  2099	            {
  2100	                "round": str(round_dir),
  2101	                "kind": "missing-scope-lock",
  2102	                "detail": f"{scope_lock} does not exist",
  2103	            }
  2104	        )
  2105	    else:
  2106	        spans = extract_allowed_spans(scope_lock.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
  2107	        if not spans:
  2108	            violations.append(
  2109	                {
  2110	                    "round": str(round_dir),
  2111	                    "kind": "unparseable-allowed-changes",
  2112	                    "detail": f"no backtick path spans found under '## Allowed Changes' in {scope_lock}",
  2113	                }
  2114	            )
  2115	        # PR-3 §4 OUT list ("Review: 与 scope-lock 的 Allowed Changes 永不接受
  2116	        # alias"): a reference root is never an authorization to write --
  2117	        # scope-lock's Allowed Changes must name project-versionable paths
  2118	        # only. Triggered *only* for a span whose alias is actually
  2119	        # declared (facet 1 P2's rejected all-`@`-spans filter would have
  2120	        # produced a false-red for e.g. a JS monorepo's `@scope/pkg/` span
  2121	        # that has nothing to do with reference roots); an undeclared
  2122	        # `@@foo/...` span is left to whatever the existing lexical
  2123	        # scope-lock matching already does with it (silently never
  2124	        # matching any real file — not a new failure mode). Reported
  2125	        # loudly, never silently dropped from `spans`.
  2126	        for span in spans:
  2127	            span_alias_match = ALIAS_CITATION_RE.match(span)
  2128	            if span_alias_match and span_alias_match.group(1) in roots:
  2129	                violations.append(
  2130	                    {
  2131	                        "round": str(round_dir),
  2132	                        "kind": "scope-lock-span-names-reference-root",
  2133	                        "detail": (
  2134	                            f"{scope_lock} names `{span}` in Allowed Changes, which "
  2135	                            f"targets declared reference root '{span_alias_match.group(1)}' -- "
  2136	                            "a reference root can never be authorized for writes"
  2137	                        ),
  2138	                    }
  2139	                )
  2140	
  2141	    if checked_files and spans:
  2142	        coverage["rule_a_files"] = len(checked_files)
  2143	        for file_path in checked_files:
  2144	            # G17 item 3: two orthogonal conditions, both required — never
  2145	            # OR, never one standing in for the other (see `_is_contained`'s
  2146	            # docstring, "all four must share this one definition"):
  2147	            #   - `is_under(...)` is the existing *lexical* scope-lock
  2148	            #     authorization — is this path string inside a span the
  2149	            #     round's scope-lock allows?
  2150	            #   - `_is_contained(file_path, project)` is *canonical*
  2151	            #     containment — does this path's real (symlink-resolved)
  2152	            #     target still land inside the project at all?
  2153	            # A file can satisfy the first while failing the second — e.g.
  2154	            # `reviews/ext.md` is lexically under `reviews/` (scope-lock
  2155	            # authorizes it) while its real target is a symlink escape out
  2156	            # of the project (fixture A). Without the AND, that file passes
  2157	            # Rule A silently even though its content is never really
  2158	            # in-project. Both conditions stay independently load-bearing:
  2159	            # dropping either one back to OR reopens a different escape
  2160	            # (scope-lock's existing project-external-path fixtures still
  2161	            # rely on the lexical half alone).
  2162	            allowed = any(
  2163	                is_under(file_path, base / span)
  2164	                for base in bases
  2165	                for span in spans
  2166	            ) and _is_contained(file_path, project)
  2167	            if not allowed:
  2168	                violations.append(
  2169	                    {
  2170	                        "round": str(round_dir),
  2171	                        "kind": "scope-lock-violation",
  2172	                        "detail": f"{file_path} is outside every allowed path in {scope_lock}",
  2173	                    }
  2174	                )
  2175	
  2176	    reviews_container = sub_containers["reviews"]
  2177	    if reviews_container is not None and reviews_container.is_dir():
  2178	        for review in [f for f in sub_files["reviews"] if f.suffix == ".md"]:
  2179	            coverage["rule_b_files"] += 1
  2180	            cited_list, exempt_external, ignored_explicit, shape_dropped, has_ignore = pathish_citations(
  2181	                review.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
  2182	            )
  2183	            coverage["citations_exempt_external"] += exempt_external
  2184	            coverage["citations_ignored_explicit"] += ignored_explicit
  2185	            coverage["citations_shape_dropped"] += shape_dropped
  2186	            if has_ignore:
  2187	                coverage["review_files_with_ignore"] += 1
  2188	            for cited in cited_list:
  2189	                coverage["citations_checked"] += 1
  2190	
  2191	                # PR-3 §2.4: domain is decided by text + the declared alias
  2192	                # set, before any filesystem access. A declared alias is
  2193	                # resolved *only* within its own root -- never against
  2194	                # `citation_bases`, never through `suffix_index` (G13/G14).
  2195	                # An alias-shaped-but-undeclared span (G15) falls straight
  2196	                # through to the unchanged project-domain block below; the
  2197	                # only difference there is a display-only hint naming the
  2198	                # declared aliases, appended to the same `dangling-citation`
  2199	                # it would have produced anyway.
  2200	                alias_match = ALIAS_CITATION_RE.match(cited)
  2201	                if alias_match and alias_match.group(1) in roots:
  2202	                    alias = alias_match.group(1)
  2203	                    root = roots[alias]
  2204	                    coverage["external_citations_checked"] += 1
  2205	                    if not root.available:
  2206	                        coverage["external_citations_unverifiable"] += 1
  2207	                        violations.append(
  2208	                            {
  2209	                                "round": str(round_dir),
  2210	                                "kind": "external-citation-unverifiable",
  2211	                                "detail": (
  2212	                                    f"{review} cites `{cited}` but reference root '{alias}' "
  2213	                                    f"is unavailable ({root.unavailable_reason}); run with "
  2214	                                    "--show-root-paths for the local path"
  2215	                                ),
  2216	                            }
  2217	                        )
  2218	                        continue
  2219	                    outcome = _resolve_external_with_locator(root, alias_match.group(2), cited)
  2220	                    if outcome == "resolved":
  2221	                        coverage["external_citations_resolved"] += 1
  2222	                    elif outcome == "not_found":
  2223	                        coverage["external_citations_not_found"] += 1
  2224	                        violations.append(
  2225	                            {
  2226	                                "round": str(round_dir),
  2227	                                "kind": "external-citation-not-found",
  2228	                                "detail": (
  2229	                                    f"{review} cites `{cited}` which does not exist under "
  2230	                                    f"reference root '{alias}'"
  2231	                                ),
  2232	                            }
  2233	                        )
  2234	                    else:
  2235	                        coverage["external_citations_rejected"] += 1
  2236	                        violations.append(
  2237	                            {
  2238	                                "round": str(round_dir),
  2239	                                "kind": "external-citation-rejected",
  2240	                                "detail": (
  2241	                                    f"{review} cites `{cited}` which reference root '{alias}' "
  2242	                                    "rejects (traversal, symlink escape, or outside its "
  2243	                                    "declared subpaths)"
  2244	                                ),
  2245	                            }
  2246	                        )
  2247	                    continue
  2248	
  2249	                want_dir = cited.endswith("/")
  2250	                resolved = _any_base_resolves(cited, citation_bases, project, want_dir)
  2251	                stripped = cited
  2252	                if not resolved:
  2253	                    stripped = strip_locator_suffix(cited)
  2254	                    if stripped != cited:
  2255	                        resolved = _any_base_resolves(stripped, citation_bases, project, want_dir)
  2256	                # T-064: the suffix-unique fallback no longer resolves a
  2257	                # citation (see module docstring, "Suffix hint" section) — a
  2258	                # citation with no explicit-base resolution is
  2259	                # `dangling-citation` unconditionally. What the fallback
  2260	                # still contributes, when it finds a unique and still-real
  2261	                # suffix hit, is a display-only pointer appended to this
  2262	                # violation's `detail`, plus a `citations_suffix_hinted`
  2263	                # coverage tick — neither changes whether this branch runs.
  2264	                if not resolved:
  2265	                    hint = ""
  2266	                    hint_target = suffix_hint_target(stripped, suffix_index, project)
  2267	                    if hint_target is not None:
  2268	                        coverage["citations_suffix_hinted"] += 1
  2269	                        hint_rel = hint_target.relative_to(project).as_posix()
  2270	                        hint = (
  2271	                            f" — a unique suffix match exists at {hint_rel}; if that is "
  2272	                            "the intended file, cite it by a resolvable path or mark the "
  2273	                            f"line {IGNORE_MARKER}"
  2274	                        )
  2275	                    elif alias_match is not None:
  2276	                        # G15: this span was alias-*shaped* but its alias was
  2277	                        # never declared -- resolved (or not) exactly like
  2278	                        # any other project-relative string above; this is a
  2279	                        # display-only note, not a different verdict.
  2280	                        declared = ", ".join(sorted(roots)) if roots else "(none)"
  2281	                        hint = (
  2282	                            f" — `@@{alias_match.group(1)}` is not a declared "
  2283	                            f"reference-root alias; declared: {declared}"
  2284	                        )
  2285	                    violations.append(
  2286	                        {
  2287	                            "round": str(round_dir),
  2288	                            "kind": "dangling-citation",
  2289	                            "detail": f"{review} cites `{cited}` which does not exist{hint}",
  2290	                        }
  2291	                    )
  2292	
  2293	    # E4: same-file enum contradiction in decision.md. Deliberately the
  2294	    # narrowest possible check — two enum lines in one file, compared after
  2295	    # strip().lower(). No prose parsing, no path resolution, no cross-file
  2350	        "rule_b_files": 0,
  2351	        "citations_checked": 0,
  2352	        "citations_exempt_external": 0,
  2353	        "citations_suffix_hinted": 0,
  2354	        "citations_ignored_explicit": 0,
  2355	        "citations_shape_dropped": 0,
  2356	        "review_files_with_ignore": 0,
  2357	        "rounds_review_declared": 0,
  2358	        "rounds_review_none": 0,
  2359	        "rounds_review_missing_fields": 0,
  2360	        "rounds_review_digest_declared": 0,
  2361	        # PR-3 (external-citation-base-spec-20260727.md §2.7): the two
  2362	        # `external_roots_*` fields are project-level and assigned exactly
  2363	        # once by `verify_project`, *after* its round loop -- never
  2364	        # accumulated per round. They live in this same dict (so G18's
  2365	        # coverage-key <-> SKILL.md IN-column check still sees them, and so
  2366	        # every round's local `coverage = _empty_coverage()` has the keys
  2367	        # too) but every round leaves them at 0; `verify_project`'s
  2368	        # per-round accumulation loop therefore only ever adds 0 to them,
  2369	        # and the real values are plain-assigned once after that loop.
  2370	        "external_roots_declared": 0,
  2371	        "external_roots_available": 0,
  2372	        "external_citations_checked": 0,
  2373	        "external_citations_resolved": 0,
  2374	        "external_citations_not_found": 0,
  2375	        "external_citations_rejected": 0,
  2376	        "external_citations_unverifiable": 0,
  2377	    }
  2378	
  2379	
  2380	def verify_project(project: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], dict]:
  2381	    goals_dir = project / ".harnessloop" / "goals"
  2382	    coverage = _empty_coverage()
  2383	    if not goals_dir.is_dir():
  2384	        return [], coverage
  2385	    violations: list[dict] = []
  2386	
  2387	    # G17 item 1 (external-citation-base-spec-20260727.md §3.1): the
  2388	    # container chain is checked top-down, level by level, *before* the
  2389	    # next level down is ever listed — `goals_dir` itself here, then each
  2390	    # `goal_dir`, then each `round_dir` below. `evidence`/`reviews` are the
  2391	    # two remaining levels, checked inside `verify_round` once a clean
  2392	    # `round_dir` reaches it. This replaces the previous single
  2393	    # `goals_dir.glob("*/rounds/*")` walk (which would transparently follow
  2394	    # a symlink at any of these levels the moment it opened the directory)
  2395	    # with explicit per-level iteration so each level can be
  2396	    # containment-checked before it is opened at all — a goal or round
  2397	    # directory that is itself a symlink escape (fixture C:
  2398	    # `rounds/0001 -> <outside>`) is reported and skipped without ever
  2399	    # calling `.iterdir()` / `.rglob()` on it, including the round's own
  2400	    # `scope-lock.md` and `decision.md` (fixture C's repro showed the whole
  2401	    # round, scope-lock included, being read from outside the project).
  2402	    # This check stays first and its early return unconditional (PR-3 must
  2403	    # not touch this G17 invariant): if the goals directory itself escapes,
  2404	    # nothing else in the project -- including reference-root declarations
  2405	    # -- is read.
  2406	    escape = _container_escape_violation(goals_dir, project, goals_dir)
  2407	    if escape is not None:
  2408	        return [escape], coverage
  2409	
  2410	    # PR-3: load declared external reference roots once per run (G5: every
  2411	    # run re-validates, never "validated once and trusted"). `roots` is
  2412	    # threaded into every `verify_round` call below so each round resolves
  2413	    # its `@@alias/...` citations against the same, single load. Violations
  2414	    # from the declaration itself (G1/G2/G4/G5/G6) are project-level, added
  2415	    # once here; `external-root-unavailable` (G7) is added once per
  2416	    # unavailable alias, also project-level, immediately below.
  2417	    roots, root_violations = load_reference_roots(project, verify_identity=True)
  2418	    violations.extend(root_violations)
  2419	    for alias, root in roots.items():
  2420	        if not root.available:
  2421	            violations.append(
  2422	                {
  2423	                    "round": str(project),
  2424	                    "kind": "external-root-unavailable",
  2425	                    "detail": (
  2426	                        f"reference root '{alias}' is unavailable "
  2427	                        f"({root.unavailable_reason}); every citation using this alias "
  2428	                        "will be reported external-citation-unverifiable"
  2429	                    ),
  2430	                }
  2431	            )
  2432	
  2433	    # Built once per project run (not per round/citation) — see
  2434	    # `build_suffix_index` for why this matters for performance.
  2435	    suffix_index = build_suffix_index(project)
  2436	    for goal_dir in sorted(p for p in goals_dir.iterdir() if p.is_dir()):
  2437	        escape = _container_escape_violation(goal_dir, project, goal_dir)
  2438	        if escape is not None:
  2439	            violations.append(escape)
  2440	            continue
  2441	        rounds_dir = goal_dir / "rounds"
  2442	        if not rounds_dir.is_dir():
  2443	            continue
  2444	        for round_dir in sorted(rounds_dir.iterdir()):
  2445	            escape = _container_escape_violation(round_dir, project, round_dir)
  2446	            if escape is not None:
  2447	                violations.append(escape)
  2448	                continue
  2449	            if not round_dir.is_dir():
  2450	                continue
  2451	            round_violations, round_coverage = verify_round(project, round_dir, suffix_index, roots)
  2452	            violations.extend(round_violations)
  2453	            for key in coverage:
  2454	                coverage[key] += round_coverage[key]
  2455	
  2456	    # PR-3 §2.7: project-level, single assignment *after* the round loop --
  2457	    # every round's own `coverage["external_roots_*"]` stayed 0 (see
  2458	    # `_empty_coverage`'s comment), so the accumulation loop above added 0
  2459	    # to these keys regardless of round count; this plain assignment is the
  2460	    # only place they are ever set, so they are never multiplied by the
  2461	    # number of rounds.
  2462	    coverage["external_roots_declared"] = len(roots)
  2463	    coverage["external_roots_available"] = sum(1 for r in roots.values() if r.available)
  2464	    return violations, coverage
  2465	
  2466	
  2467	def main() -> int:
  2468	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
  2469	        description=(
  2470	            "Verify mechanical Harnessloop protocol gates (scope-lock containment "
  2471	            "and citation existence). Citation spans that are regex/glob patterns, "
  2472	            "bare-domain URLs (docs.python.org/..., github.com/...), or templated "
  2473	            "paths with an angle-bracket placeholder (goals/<id>/...) are exempt "
  2474	            "automatically; a citation known to be intentional prose rather than a "
  2475	            "real reference can be exempted explicitly by putting "
  2476	            "'<!-- verify:ignore -->' on the same line or the line before it "
  2477	            "(counted in the citations_ignored_explicit / review_files_with_ignore "
  2478	            "coverage fields, so ignore-marker use is monitorable rather than "
  2479	            "silent). A pathish span containing '/' whose tail has no extension, "
  2480	            "no trailing '/', and no '..' segment is silently dropped before "
  2481	            "existence checking; counted in citations_shape_dropped. "
  2482	            "Citations are resolved against the project root, the round's goal and "
  2483	            "round directories, the project's own .harnessloop/ directory (for "
  2484	            "citations using a PATHISH_PREFIXES prefix verbatim, e.g. "
  2485	            "setup/data-sources.md), and the root of every git submodule (any depth) "
  2486	            "declared in the project's .gitmodules (canonical-containment-checked: a path "
  2487	            "or .gitmodules entry that would resolve outside the project — including via "
  2488	            "a project-internal symlink or a symlink-then-'..' round-trip — is never "
  2489	            "treated as resolved). A trailing :<line>, :<start>-<end> (optionally repeated "
  2490	            "as a comma-separated multi-range, e.g. :44-46,443-507), or ::<anchor> "
  2491	            "locator is stripped before checking. A citation still unresolved after all "
  2492	            "of the above is reported as dangling-citation unconditionally (T-064: a "
  2493	            "path-suffix match is no longer a resolution path); if that citation has "
  2494	            ">=2 path segments and matches exactly one file in the project's "
  2495	            "tracked-plus-untracked-not-ignored worktree index (or, outside a git "
  2496	            "working tree, the walked and noise-pruned tree) as a path suffix, and that "
  2497	            "matched path still actually exists, its detail carries a display-only hint "
  2498	            "pointing at the match (counted in the citations_suffix_hinted coverage "
  2499	            "field) — the citation still fails. A citation ending in / must resolve to "
  2500	            "a directory. Home-relative (~/...), filesystem-absolute (/...), and "
  2501	            "Windows-absolute (C:/..., \\\\server\\share...) citations are exempt (out "
  2502	            "of project scope; counted in the citations_exempt_external coverage field)."
  2503	        )
  2504	    )
  2505	    parser.add_argument("--project", "-p", default=".", help="Target project directory. Defaults to current directory.")
  2506	    parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", help="Print machine-readable JSON output.")
  2507	    # PR-3 §2.7 safety constraint: violation detail, the coverage line, and
  2508	    # --json never print a reference root's local path (G20). This is a
  2509	    # separate, additional, human-output-only section -- deliberately not
  2510	    # a verdict-changing knob (G19: this repo's argparse must never grow an
  2511	    # allow-missing/skip-roots/no-external-style option that would let an
  2512	    # unavailable or rejected root quietly stop failing); it has no effect
  2513	    # at all under --json, so the JSON schema whitelist stays exactly what
  2514	    # it was without this flag.
  2515	    parser.add_argument(
  2516	        "--show-root-paths",
  2517	        action="store_true",
  2518	        help=(
  2519	            "Also print each declared reference-root alias's raw local path "
  2520	            "(from .harnessloop/local/reference-roots.local.json), for "
  2521	            "debugging an unavailable/rejected root. Human output only; "
  2522	            "never changes exit code, violations, or coverage, and has no "
  2523	            "effect under --json."
  2524	        ),
  2525	    )
  2526	    args = parser.parse_args()
  2527	
  2528	    project = Path(args.project).resolve()
  2529	    if not project.is_dir():
  2530	        print(f"Project directory not found: {project}", file=sys.stderr)
  2531	        return 2
  2532	
  2533	    violations, coverage = verify_project(project)
  2534	    if args.json:
  2535	        print(
  2536	            json.dumps(
  2537	                {"project": str(project), "violations": violations, "coverage": coverage},
  2538	                indent=2,
  2539	                ensure_ascii=False,
  2540	            )
  2541	        )
  2542	    else:
  2543	        print(f"Harnessloop verify: {project}")
  2544	        if violations:
  2545	            for violation in violations:
  2546	                print(f"  [{violation['kind']}] {violation['detail']}")
  2547	            print(f"{len(violations)} violation(s) found.")
  2548	        elif coverage["rounds_zero_inspected"] > 0:
  2549	            # E2(b): a clean exit is not a blanket "all clear" when some
  2550	            # rounds had nothing under evidence/ or reviews/ to check in the
  2551	            # first place. Print the qualified form instead of the
  2552	            # unqualified banner below so a clean exit for those rounds
  2553	            # cannot be misread as "checked and clean".
  2554	            print(
  2555	                "passed, but not a clean sweep: "
  2556	                f"{coverage['rounds_zero_inspected']} of {coverage['rounds']} round(s) had "
  2557	                "nothing under evidence/ or reviews/ to inspect — a clean exit for those "
  2558	                'rounds means "nothing to check", not "checked and clean".'
  2559	            )
  2560	        else:
  2561	            print("All mechanical protocol gates passed.")
  2562	        # Unconditional coverage line (E2(b)): printed regardless of pass/fail
  2563	        # so "was this gate actually run over anything" is always visible,
  2564	        # not just inferable from exit code. Field names here are the short
  2565	        # print-form of the `coverage` dict keys (see `_empty_coverage`);
  2566	        # the --json output uses the full dict verbatim.
  2567	        print(
  2568	            "coverage: "
  2569	            f"rounds={coverage['rounds']} rule_a_files={coverage['rule_a_files']} "
  2570	            f"rule_b_files={coverage['rule_b_files']} citations={coverage['citations_checked']} "
  2571	            f"citations_exempt_external={coverage['citations_exempt_external']} "
  2572	            f"citations_suffix_hinted={coverage['citations_suffix_hinted']} "
  2573	            f"citations_ignored_explicit={coverage['citations_ignored_explicit']} "
  2574	            f"citations_shape_dropped={coverage['citations_shape_dropped']} "
  2575	            f"review_files_with_ignore={coverage['review_files_with_ignore']} "
  2576	            f"zero_inspected={coverage['rounds_zero_inspected']} "
  2577	            f"review_declared={coverage['rounds_review_declared']} "
  2578	            f"review_none={coverage['rounds_review_none']} "
  2579	            f"review_missing_fields={coverage['rounds_review_missing_fields']} "
  2580	            f"review_digest_declared={coverage['rounds_review_digest_declared']} "
  2581	            f"external_roots_declared={coverage['external_roots_declared']} "
  2582	            f"external_roots_available={coverage['external_roots_available']} "
  2583	            f"external_citations_checked={coverage['external_citations_checked']} "
  2584	            f"external_citations_resolved={coverage['external_citations_resolved']} "
  2585	            f"external_citations_not_found={coverage['external_citations_not_found']} "
  2586	            f"external_citations_rejected={coverage['external_citations_rejected']} "
  2587	            f"external_citations_unverifiable={coverage['external_citations_unverifiable']}"
  2588	        )
  2589	        if args.show_root_paths:
  2590	            # Deliberately the *only* place a reference root's local path is
  2591	            # ever printed (G20 pins violation detail / coverage line /
  2592	            # --json to alias-only; this flag is the documented escape
  2593	            # valve those three point at, and it prints nothing else).
  2594	            bindings, _local_error = _load_local_bindings(project / REFERENCE_ROOTS_LOCAL_REL)
  2595	            _roots, _violations = load_reference_roots(project, verify_identity=True)
  2596	            print("reference root local paths (--show-root-paths):")
  2597	            if not _roots:
  2598	                print("  (no reference roots declared)")
  2599	            for alias in sorted(_roots):
  2600	                raw = bindings.get(alias)
  2601	                print(f"  {alias}: {raw if raw else '(unbound)'}")
  2602	    return 1 if violations else 0
  2603

  2605	if __name__ == "__main__":
  2606	    raise SystemExit(main())
